## Semantic Chunks
### Section: Introduction
- **Level**: `intro`
- **Hash**: `dc9fad8fcdbec7c62a323f19c4cb9ef5ac4151228bcaeb9e6e4cd0518aba633d`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-teachers-tell-when-a-student-pasted-text-into-an-essay` (Similarity: 0.9178)

```text
Identifying that a paste event occurred is only the first step: determining the exact origin of the pasted text—whether from the web, a peer, a generative AI model, or an authentic offline draft—is critical for fair evaluation.

When an instructor notices that 800 words appeared in a student's document in a single second, the critical question becomes: "Where did this text originate?" Did the student copy a paragraph from an academic journal, purchase an essay from an online paper mill, receive text from a classmate via Discord, generate it with ChatGPT, or simply paste their own notes from Microsoft Word?

Through Checkmark Plagiarism's Paste + Source Matching engine, instructors can isolate the raw paste payload and cross-reference it across billions of web pages, peer student databases, and advanced AI detection models in seconds.

Checkmark Plagiarism powers source matching by pairing essay writing playback with AI detection , plagiarism detection , autograding , and integrations with Canvas and Google Classroom .
```

### Section: The 4 Potential Origins of a Pasted Text Block
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `767f6a961f157c40f410dd45c94612d791444b5de75c35aa156d3c617495d566`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-does-a-large-paste-event-mean-in-a-student-essay` (Similarity: 0.8518)

```text
1. Web & Academic Database Sources

Text copied from Wikipedia, JSTOR articles, Chegg, Course Hero, or online blogs without proper quotation marks.

2. Peer-to-Peer Student Collusion

Text copied from a current classmate's document or an archived essay from a previous semester.

3. Generative AI Output

Text generated by ChatGPT, Claude, or Gemini containing synthetic statistical markers and hallucinated citations.

4. Student's Authentic Offline Draft

Text composed independently in Microsoft Word, Apple Pages, or personal handwritten notes and pasted into the LMS.
```

### Section: How Checkmark Paste + Source Matching Solves the Puzzle
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `30b0f6ee831a24bc6cfdf9ae7ff8e63a87e75da51ee0a9dfdb66c0d236f36a7f`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/a-student-pasted-half-their-essay-does-that-prove-plagiarism` (Similarity: 0.8879)

```text
Rather than guessing, Checkmark Plagiarism isolates the exact raw pasted string and subjects it to targeted forensic analysis:

Billion-Webpage Index Search: Automatically searches global internet archives and scholarly databases to locate exact matching URL sources. 
 Institutional Peer Cross-Checking: Scans the institutional repository to determine if the exact paste string appears in another student's submission. 
 Targeted AI Probability Analysis: Runs the isolated paste block through dual static and writing playback AI neural engines to evaluate perplexity and burstiness. 
 Original Paste Preservation: Displays the unedited text as it existed at the microsecond of insertion, bypassing any downstream synonym swaps.

Read more in how Checkmark writing process analysis works .
```

### Section: Diagnostic Matrix: Identifying the Source of Pasted Text
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `1362cb74bcec4183093f0b67b3ed29eefb80cb845dbda80dacfc79a009091680`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-evidence-should-teachers-collect-when-they-suspect-ai-use` (Similarity: 0.8521)

```text
Plagiarized Web / Peer Source

Plagiarism engine shows high direct match % with specific URL. 
 Citations point to real, existing academic articles. 
 Contains formatting artifacts (hidden web links, odd font styles).

Generative AI (ChatGPT) Source

Plagiarism engine shows 0% direct match (text is novel). 
 AI detector flags 95%+ probability; low burstiness score. 
 Citations contain hallucinated authors, fake DOIs, or phantom journals.
```

### Section: A 5-Step Educator Source Identification Protocol
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `44c807c7b0296c469cf91781149ed9e3286819aec28dcc8b9793e73a38adea33`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-i-find-the-original-source-a-student-copied-from` (Similarity: 0.9185)

```text
Educator Source Tracing Checklist:

1. Open the essay writing playback report in Canvas or Google Classroom. 
 2. Click the highlighted paste event to isolate the raw pasted text payload. 
 3. Review the Checkmark Plagiarism Source Match report for direct web or peer links. 
 4. Check the AI Detection breakdown for that specific pasted segment. 
 5. Verify cited bibliography entries in Google Scholar to confirm if sources are real or fabricated.
```
